**New (2026-07-03):** [[sources/ellis-the-great-contradiction-2025|Joseph J. Ellis, *The Great Contradiction: The Tragic Side of the American Founding* (Knopf, 2025)]] — interpretive synthesis on the "downside" of the American founding: the avoidable (Shakespearean) tragedies of failing to end slavery and failing to avoid Indian removal. Prioritizes slavery's impact on the Declaration, 1787 Convention compromises (three-fifths, fugitive slave, slave trade), and founders' private regrets vs. public choices. Washington’s 1799 will (Mount Vernon manumission, education, Freetown, Billy Lee) as singular among VA planters; Jefferson’s bimodal views (*Notes*, autobiography) and expatriation hostage; 1790 petitions/Madison closure of the window; 1790 Census demographics; parallel Knox-McGillivray Treaty of New York (1790) and demography trumping diplomacy. **Deployed Subagent Strategy**: main-thread scaffold (source + Section Plan + pre-created anchors: constitutional-convention-1787, mount-vernon, slavery-in-the-american-founding concept, 3 NEW from subagent proposals); 3 disjoint narrative caches (~6k body lines); 3 Sonnet subagents. R02/R03 rich; R01 recovered on main from cache. Main reconciliation (Read-before-Edit); 0 *new* broken links (global total 25 pre-existing only; obvious typos in our files fixed); schema clean; alias sync clean for new names. **6 new pages** (source; events/constitutional-convention-1787 + treaty-of-new-york-1790; places/mount-vernon; concepts/slavery-in-the-american-founding; actors/knox-henry + mcgillivray-alexander). **12+ updated** (washington-george and jefferson-thomas with will/Notes/auction details + quotes; american-revolution + constitutional-convention with Historiography and compromises; age-of-expansion and long-19th-century coverage notes + counts; source). Valuable gap-filler for north-america / Age of Expansion / founding contradictions. Source .txt filed to raw/4. Modern Times/. Co-Authored-By: main thread + 3 Sonnet subagents.

Shirer, *The Rise and Fall of the Third Reich* (Simon & Schuster, 1960)]] — the landmark journalist-historian synthesis of Nazi Germany (~1,600 pp., National Book Award), written from the captured German archives (Foreign Office, OKW, Halder/Goebbels/Jodl diaries, Nuremberg record) plus Shirer's own Berlin eyewitness years 1934–40. **New (2026-07-03):** [[sources/fehrman-this-vast-enterprise-2026|Craig Fehrman, *This Vast Enterprise: A New History of Lewis and Clark* (Avid Reader Press / Simon & Schuster, 2026)]] — modern corrective narrative reframing the Corps of Discovery (1804–1806) as a "vast enterprise" (Clark) driven by land motives for all parties (Natives + Americans + Jefferson), with rotating POV chapters centering ensemble cast (York, Sacagawea, Black Buffalo, Ordway, Coboway, Piahito, Wolf Calf, etc.) over captains-alone myth. Strong on pre-existing Native power (Lakota tolls/checkpoints), Sacagawea agency (pirogue rescue, Shoshone reunion/sign ID, whale demand), York prominence (hunting/rapids/vote), diplomatic limits (Piahito/Sheheke delegations), Marias/Great Falls/return fight realities, Lewis 1809 suicide, Clark treaties (~419M acres), publication (Biddle 1814), and literary/myth legacy. **Deployed Subagent Strategy**: main-thread scaffold (source page + Section Plan + 15+ anchor pages pre-created before agents: event, 11+ actors, 3 places, 2 bio hubs, period update); 5 disjoint caches (~15k-line file); 5 Sonnet subagents (background) with exclusive ranges + strict established-names list + verbatim grounding only. All succeeded. Main reconciliation (Read-before-Edit); 0 new broken links; schema clean. **~12 new pages** (source; event lewis-and-clark-expedition-1804; actors lewis-meriwether/clark-william/sacagawea/york-enslaved/black-buffalo/the-partisan/ordway-john/coboway/piahito/wolf-calf/sheheke; places st-louis/camp-dubois/fort-mandan; bio hubs for captains). **15+ updated** (period long-19th-century coverage note + sources count; cross-links; index/log). Source filed to raw/4. Modern Times/. Valuable gap-filler for north-america / Long 19th Century. Co-Authored-By: main thread + 5 Sonnet subagents (R01–R05).

**New (2026-07-03):** [[sources/williamson-price-of-democracy-2025|Vanessa S. Williamson, *The Price of Democracy: The Revolutionary Power of Taxation in American History* (Basic Books, 2025)]] — argument-driven history showing that U.S. tax fights have been fights over the breadth of democracy. Challenges the "born antitax" myth (Tea Party opposed corporate bailout/tax cut for EIC; colonists wanted power to tax themselves). "Free countries are high-tax countries." Part One: Stamp Act, Shays (catalyst for Constitution's insulated fiscal design), Whiskey. Part Two (main-thread): Radical Reconstruction tax policies for schools/land (Black legislators like Matthew Gaines: "I like to pay taxes on it. It’s my privilege"), violent "Redemption" via "taxpayer" leagues and paramilitaries, Jim Crow as taxpayer rule. Part Three: 16th Amendment, FDR mass tax/"conscription of wealth", Reagan/antitax triumph, "tax eaters," democratic erosion. **Deployed Subagent Strategy** (main scaffold + pre-created anchors; 2 Sonnet on light ranges; Range 02 dense Part Two on main per atrocity triage). **9 new pages:** 6 events (stamp-act-crisis-1765, boston-tea-party-1773, shays-rebellion-1786-1787, whiskey-rebellion-1794, sixteenth-amendment-1913, proposition-13-california-1978); 3 concepts (taxation-and-representation, antitax-politics, taxpayer-identity). Heavy updates to [[events/american-civil-war-1861-1865]] (tax subsection), [[actors/united-states]] (fiscal foundations), [[processes/new-deal]], source. All events have Historiography; 0 new broken links. Source filed.

**New (2026-07-02):** [[sources/hayek-fatal-conceit-1988|F. A. Hayek, *The Fatal Conceit: The Errors of Socialism* (1988)]] — eleventh item of the fascism-studies/US-political-thought batch and direct capstone companion to the *Road to Serfdom* (2026-07-01) and *Individualism and Economic Order* (2026-07-02) ingests: Hayek’s late manifesto on the fatal conceit of constructivist rationalism, the spontaneous extended order, cultural evolution via group selection, and the demographic consequences of destroying several property and abstract rules. Deployed-subagent ingest (3 Sonnet agents over chapter-aligned ranges). **7 new pages:** [[concepts/fatal-conceit]] (the title thesis + the four constructivist requirements + population-survival implication), [[concepts/extended-order]] (spontaneous macro-order of cooperation; prices as dispersed-knowledge discovery procedure), [[concepts/several-property]] (Maine’s term; foundation of trade, liberty, and civilisation), [[concepts/constructivist-rationalism]] (Descartes–Rousseau–scientism; the four tests traditional morals fail), [[concepts/cultural-evolution]] (group selection on rules via comparative population/wealth success; between instinct and reason; “symbolic truths”), [[controversies/was-socialism-a-mistake]] (factual vs. moral dispute with three positions), plus the source page (full Section Plan + enriched Claims Integrated with verbatim grounding from all ranges). **5 updated:** [[actors/hayek-friedrich]] (new “Late Capstone” section: personal notes on escape from rationalism, agnostic concession, catallaxy proposal, “we owe our morals… to the acceptance of such scientifically unacceptable factual claims,” duty to resist destruction of the order), prior Hayek concepts (cross-links), index, log. Key verbatim theses: the extended order “arose from unintentionally conforming to certain traditional and largely moral practices”; socialism “endangers the standard of living and the life itself of a large proportion of our existing population”; religion preserved counter-instinctual rules; “in the long run we are all dead” contrasted with the survival stakes. 0 new broken links; schema-clean; alias sync run. Off-list source; filed to raw/4. Modern Times/. Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 + 3 Sonnet subagents (A–C).

**New (2026-07-01):** [[sources/paxton-vichy-france-1972|Robert O. Paxton, *Vichy France: Old Guard and New Order, 1940–1944* (Knopf, 1972)]] — the landmark monograph that remade Vichy historiography from the captured German archives, and the first of the fascism-studies batch (Paxton/Griffin/Payne/Evans + Hayek/Flynn/Whitman now in raw/). Core findings: collaboration was a **French initiative** repeatedly offered to an indifferent Germany ("who was the suitor and who was the wooed"; Pétain as "Marshal Bitte," not "Marshal Nein"); the National Revolution was **indigenous French reaction**, including anti-Jewish laws enacted without German demand; and on comparative measures the "shield" protected nothing ("A gauleiter would have made many Frenchmen suffer; in the end he might have gotten less"). **10 new pages:** [[actors/vichy-france]], [[actors/laval-pierre]], [[actors/darlan-francois]], [[actors/de-gaulle-charles]], [[events/franco-german-armistice-1940]], [[events/vichy-anti-jewish-laws-1940]], [[processes/national-revolution-vichy]], [[concepts/collaboration-and-collaborationism]], [[controversies/vichy-shield-thesis]], plus the source page. **6 updated:** [[actors/petain-philippe]] (full Vichy chapter joining his WWI career), [[events/fall-of-france-1940]] (armistice-as-choice), [[events/operation-torch-1942]], [[events/the-holocaust]] (France section with Paxton's responsibility reframing), [[controversies/fascism-and-the-political-left]] (Paxton's primary-text fascism definition — his conservative/fascist spectrum and double verdict on Vichy — plus the Left-at-Vichy evidence both positions cite). Deployed-subagent ingest; one range recovered on main thread after a content-filter block. 0 broken links. Filed to raw/4. Modern Times/.

**New (2026-07-01):** [[queries/where-does-fascism-fall-on-the-political-spectrum|Query: Where does fascism fall on the political spectrum?]] — filed curator–assistant analysis following the D'Souza ingest: the two-axes framing (state–individual vs. egalitarianism–hierarchy), the egalitarian-pretense argument (Djilas, Orwell) and its limits (victim selection, recruitment reach, defection patterns), international vs. national socialism, August 1914 as the falsification event of the Second International, the Lenin/Mussolini fork, and Gentile's actual role (post-hoc systematizer of the totalitarian state, not originator of the national turn). Positions attributed (curator's view vs. assistant's analysis) — the adjudicated counterpart to the deliberately neutral [[controversies/fascism-and-the-political-left]]. Seed outline for a future comparisons/ page on totalitarianism as a genus (Arendt/Djilas/Aly), pending the Paxton/Griffin/Payne/Evans/Sternhell and Hayek/Flynn/Hartz/Whitman acquisitions.

Thesis (Bashford & Macintyre): the founding "Australian Settlement" — tariff, arbitration, White Australia, state paternalism, imperial dependence — stressed by four crises (WWI, the Depression, WWII, the 1970s), dismantled by the Hawke–Keating reforms, and succeeded by multiculturalism and Asian engagement; Indigenous policy moves from protection through self-determination to Mabo and the apology; the closing "history anxiety" makes historiography itself first-class subject matter. 0 broken links; schema clean. Filed to raw/4. Modern Times/. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 + Sonnet subagents (R1–R8).

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/bailey-balkan-prehistory-2000|Douglass W. Bailey, *Balkan Prehistory: Exclusion, Incorporation and Identity* (Routledge, 2000)]] — first accessible English synthesis of the SE-European Neolithic & Copper Age, c. 6500–2500 BC. Off-list raw/ file (epub→txt). Conventional site/chronology backbone over a distinctive post-processual thesis: the period's revolution was in *how people arranged themselves, places and things*, not economy (vs Childe) or invasion (vs Gimbutas). **3 new pages:** concept [[concepts/exclusion-incorporation-projection|Exclusion, Incorporation and Projection]] (Bailey's interpretive triad + the "illusion" argument); place [[places/danube-gorges|The Danube Gorges]] (Lepenski Vir/Vlasac — settled life before pottery & domesticates). **7 updated:** [[places/balkans]], [[places/varna]] (mortuary display as projected "illusion"), [[processes/old-europe-copper-metallurgy]], [[periods/neolithic]], [[periods/chalcolithic]], [[concepts/neolithic-communities]], [[controversies/indo-european-origins]] (the Balkan tell-collapse: indigenous gradualism vs Kurgan invasion). 0 broken links.

**New (2026-07-08):** [[sources/laiou-morrisson-byzantine-economy-2007|Angeliki E. Laiou & Cécile Morrisson, *The Byzantine Economy* (Cambridge Medieval Textbooks, 2007)]] — the authoritative concise economic history of Byzantium (4th c.–1453), ingested via the **Deployed Subagent Strategy** (4 chapter-aligned Sonnet extractors + main-thread authorship of the interpretive comparative chapter). Governing frame: Byzantium as a **coherent pre-industrial mixed economy** — the state the dominant but never sole actor — passing from late-antique prosperity through 7th-c. crisis, state-led recovery, the 11th–12th-c. "age of accelerated growth" (as the state recedes), to post-1204 "small-state economics" and subordination to Italian commerce. **4 new:** [[processes/byzantine-economy]] (the spine), [[processes/byzantine-monetary-system]] (the *nomisma* as "dollar of the Middle Ages" → 1092 Komnenian reform → *hyperpyron* debasement → 1367 silver *stavraton*), [[concepts/premodern-mixed-economy]] (the contested thesis, held as a position), [[places/thessalonike]] (the empire's second city). **7 updated:** [[actors/byzantine-empire]], [[periods/late-antiquity]], [[periods/early-middle-ages]], [[periods/high-middle-ages]], [[periods/late-middle-ages]], [[places/constantinople]], [[processes/latin-romania-economy]]. A genuine gap-fill: the wiki previously had no dedicated Byzantine economic-history spine. 0 new broken links; schema clean. Source: [[sources/laiou-morrisson-byzantine-economy-2007]].

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/sarris-empires-of-faith-2011|Peter Sarris, *Empires of Faith: The Fall of Rome to the Rise of Islam, 500–700* (Oxford History of Medieval Europe, 2011)]] — Top_100 #74; single-author synthesis of the late-antique-to-early-medieval transformation, ingested as an **enrichment/update** in the collection's strongest period (main-thread deep reads of Sarris's distinctive arguments, honest hybrid depth). Materialist transformationist line: **[[demonetisation-of-the-post-roman-west|fiscal de-monetisation]]** of the West, a **maximalist [[justinianic-plague-scale|Justinianic plague]]**, a Eurasian/steppe frame, religion-as-state-power, and the convergence of Christian holy war into Arabia. Comparative core: West — aristocracy survives at the state's expense; 7th-c. Anatolia — state survives at the aristocracy's. **3 new:** [[processes/demonetisation-of-the-post-roman-west]], [[controversies/justinianic-plague-scale]], source page. **8 updated:** [[controversies/fall-of-rome-causes]], [[events/justinianic-plague-541]], [[actors/justinian]], [[actors/khosrow-i]], [[actors/heraclius]], [[actors/muhammad-and-the-rise-of-islam]], [[periods/late-antiquity]], [[periods/early-middle-ages]]. 0 broken links on new pages; schema clean. **New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/bostoen-brinkman-kongo-kingdom-2018|Koen Bostoen & Inge Brinkman (eds.), *The Kongo Kingdom: The Origins, Dynamics and Cosmopolitan Culture of an African Polity* (Cambridge UP, 2018)]] — the primary outcome of the ERC-funded **KongoKing project** (2012–16, Ghent University); the wiki's first **deep ingest for west-central Africa** and the most recent specialist scholarship on the [[kingdom-of-kongo]]. Cross-disciplinary: history, archaeology, historical linguistics, art history, anthropology. **Deployed Subagent Strategy** (5 Sonnet agents over disjoint chapter ranges; main thread scaffolded + reconciled). Core findings: (1) **Thornton's revised origin thesis** (ch. 1) — a multi-stage process from c. 1250 CE, with the kingdom's roots in the Seven Kingdoms (*Kongo dia Nlaza*) zone east of the Inkisi River, not a sudden founding event; (2) **Bostoen & de Schryver's Bayesian phylogeny of the Kikongo Language Cluster** (ch. 3) — 40 languages, 5 major subclades; 17th-c. Kikongo is ancestral only to the Kisikongo variety (Mbanza Kongo), not to the full KLC; KLC divergence predates the kingdom; (3) **Thornton's Soyo analysis** (ch. 4) — the da Silva dynasty systematically leveraged its constitutional kingmaker role to dismantle centralization from 1641 onward, making Soyo the proximate engine of [[kongo-decentralization]]; (4) **Fromont, Cranshof/Nikis/de Maret, Clist** (chs. 6–8) — three independent material-culture lines (Christian objects, woven-motif ceramics, tobacco pipes) all confirm Kongo as a **cosmopolitan, extraversive polity** that actively appropriated and transformed foreign goods into local prestige culture; (5) **Vos and Heywood** (chs. 10–11) — cosmopolitanism without a state: 19th-c. Kongo elites maintained Atlantic engagement through adapted external partnerships (Order of Christ, Portuguese alliance, textiles) even after political fragmentation, while Kongo identity survived intact in the Brazilian diaspora through the *congadas* tradition, grounded in the kingdom's pre-existing Christian royal culture. **25 new pages:** actors [[afonso-i-kongo]], [[joao-i-kongo]], [[lukeni-lua-nimi]], [[kimpa-vita]], [[house-of-soyo-da-silva]]; events [[kongo-origins-c1390]], [[battle-of-mbwila-1665]], [[introduction-of-tobacco-kongo]]; places [[mbanza-kongo]], [[soyo-province-kongo]], [[mbata-province-kongo]], [[nsundi-province-kongo]], [[kongo-dia-nlaza]], [[barbela-eastern-border-kongo]]; processes [[kongo-decentralization]], [[kongo-diaspora-brazil]], [[kongo-nineteenth-century-cosmopolitanism]]; concepts [[kikongo-language-cluster]], [[seventeenth-century-kikongo]], [[kongo-political-structure-1480]], [[kongo-christian-visual-culture]], [[kongo-ceramic-tradition]], [[kongo-cosmopolitanism]], [[kongo-book-culture]]; source page. **2 updated:** [[kingdom-of-kongo]] (now richly linked), [[kongo-decentralization]] (detailed da Silva phase chronology added by agent). 0 broken links (Kongo changed set; 8 pre-existing in unrelated files). Schema clean. Deployed Subagent Strategy — 5 Sonnet agents + main thread. Filed to raw/4. Modern Times/ (or appropriate GHA-series folder TBD). Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.6 + subagents (1–5).

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/halsall-warfare-society-barbarian-west-2003|Guy Halsall, *Warfare and Society in the Barbarian West, 450–900* (Routledge, 2003)]] — the standard scholarly synthesis of war in the post-Roman West, between the well-studied late-Roman army and the tenth-century world of knights and castles. Deliberately **revisionist**: warfare must be read **"substantively"** (within the society's own mentalities) not by the **"normalist"** assumption of timeless military principles; it was a form of communication with a **"grammar of warfare"**; and it **changed over time** (transformations ~600 and in the 9th c.), against the habit of treating the whole period as a static "Germanic"/"heroic" whole. Core claims: **small armies** (low thousands, not the sources' tens of thousands), **ravaging/harrying to provoke battle** as the strategic core (sieges marginal — "no instances in Merovingian Gaul where a walled town was captured by straightforward assault"), the **rout, not the clash, doing the killing** (the Eccles, Kent palaeopathology), and the shift from a **tax-funded standing army** to **obligation-based levies** as the hinge of state power — with the **Brunner/Lynn White "stirrup → shock cavalry → feudalism"** chain (and Poitiers/Tours 732 as its hinge) rejected. **Hybrid ingest** — the main thread read the analytical core (Intro + Ch1–2) and scaffolded; **4 Sonnet agents** digested the descriptive thematic chapters (raising armies; size/campaigning; weaponry/battle; siege/epilogue) in parallel; main thread synthesised. **3 new pages:** the warfare-hub thematic overview [[early-medieval-warfare|Early Medieval Warfare in the Barbarian West (c. 450–900)]] (the high-detail deliverable), concept [[grammar-of-warfare]], and the source page. **5 updated:** process [[medieval-warfare]] (early-medieval phase), period [[early-middle-ages]] (warfare section), [[controversies/causes-of-the-fall-of-the-western-roman-empire|the fall-of-Rome controversy]] (the military mechanism behind Halsall's migrations-as-result position), [[charlemagne]] (the *mansi* service thresholds), [[charles-martel]] (demolition of the stirrup/"birth of feudalism" thesis). Companion to Heather (externalist) and Ward-Perkins (material decline) in the open fall debate. Top_100 #70 ✅. 0 broken links (changed set; 5 pre-existing in unedited parts of the period/process pages); schema clean. Filed to raw/3. Middle Ages/. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 + subagents (A–D).

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/wiesehofer-ancient-persia-2001|Josef Wiesehöfer, *Ancient Persia: From 550 BC to 650 AD* (I.B. Tauris, 2001)]] — a **thematic, source-critical synthesis** of all three pre-Islamic Iranian empires ([[achaemenid-empire|Achaemenid]], [[parthian-empire|Arsacid/Parthian]], [[sasanian-empire|Sasanian]]), organized not as narrative but under a recurring grid — testimonies, kingship, administration, religion, everyday life — with a standing polemic against the Greco-Roman **"decline/decadence" topos** and modern Orientalism. The wiki's first treatment of pre-Islamic Iran *as a single subject*, and its great value-add is the under-covered **Parthian** and **Sasanian** periods plus a concluding history of the European **rediscovery** of ancient Iran. **Deployed Subagent Strategy** — four Sonnet agents over disjoint sections (Parthian; Sasanian kingship/religion; Eranshahr/reforms/fall; Conclusion/decipherment) drafted grounded digests; the main thread authored all pages. The [[achaemenid-empire|Achaemenid]] Part I was read **light-touch** (it reinforces the already-deep [[sources/briant-from-cyrus-to-alexander-2002|Briant]] scaffolding). **5 new pages:** concepts [[eranshahr|Ēranšahr — the Sasanian idea of Iran]] and [[rediscovery-of-ancient-iran|the Rediscovery of Ancient Iran]] (Niebuhr, Anquetil-Duperron, Grotefend's decipherment, the birth of Iranian Studies); actors [[kartir|Kartir (Kerdir)]] (the great Zoroastrian priest), [[khosrow-i|Khosrow I Anushirvan]] (the reforming king), [[shapur-ii|Shapur II]]. **7 updated:** [[parthian-empire]] (against "feudalism" and the "philhellene" myth — [CONTRADICTION]), [[sasanian-empire]] (Arsacid **continuity** + "**no state Church**" — [CONTRADICTION]; the late centralizing turn under Khosrow I; structural causes of the fall), [[ardashir-i]], [[shapur-i]], [[zoroastrianism]], [[manichaeism]], [[achaemenid-empire]] (light). **Dedup:** merged the duplicate `sassanid-empire.md` into the canonical [[sasanian-empire]] (aliases added; ~34 inbound links repointed) and removed the duplicate. Three theses thread the Iran cluster: **continuity over rupture**, **religion-as-politics (not a state church)**, and **late centralization under Khosrow I**. **New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/heather-fall-roman-empire-2005|Peter Heather, *The Fall of the Roman Empire: A New History of Rome and the Barbarians* (Oxford, 2005)]] — the leading modern **externalist** answer to Gibbon on why the western [[late-antiquity|Roman Empire]] fell. Heather argues the fourth-century West was a **structurally sound state**, not one in terminal internal decline, and that it was destroyed by an **exogenous shock**: the [[huns|Hunnic]] irruption that drove the Germanic "supergroups" — themselves consolidated by centuries of contact with Roman wealth, subsidy and aggression — across the frontier, followed by a **fiscal spiral** in which each lost province (above all [[vandal-kingdom|Vandal Africa]], 439) starved the army that held the empire together. His closing reversal of Gibbon: "By virtue of its unbounded aggression, Roman imperialism was ultimately responsible for its own destruction." **Light-touch hybrid ingest** of a well-trodden topic already deeply scaffolded by [[cameron-cah-v14-2000|CAH XIV]] (Heather's own chapters): the intro/conclusion and Hunnic chapters were read closely for his distinctive thesis, then spliced into the existing network. **1 new actor page** — [[attila|Attila the Hun]] (a glaring gap, and the human face of Heather's exogenous shock). **7 updated:** the [[causes-of-the-fall-of-the-western-roman-empire|fall controversy]] (position 1 + the "supergroup" mechanism), the [[fall-of-the-western-roman-empire-476|fall event (476)]], [[battle-of-adrianople-378|Adrianople (378)]] (the *true* beginning), [[huns|the Huns]], [[alaric|Alaric]], [[stilicho|Stilicho]], and period [[late-antiquity|Late Antiquity]]. Sits opposite Ward-Perkins (material-decline) and Halsall (migration-as-result) in the open fall debate. **New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/noll-canaan-israel-antiquity-2013|K. L. Noll, *Canaan and Israel in Antiquity: A Textbook on History and Religion* (2nd ed., T&T Clark 2013)]] — a methodologically self-conscious textbook of the Levant from the lithic eras to the Persian period, organised around the question "how do we know what we think we know?" Noll writes from a **moderate-skeptic** position distinct from both the minimalists and [[sources/dever-beyond-the-texts-2017|Dever's]] centrism: the Bible is a late (Iron II–Persian–Hellenistic) anthology of folklore and eponymic legend with no Bronze Age memory, and he favours a Low/Middle (radiocarbon-driven) chronology that deflates the tenth century. Two signature frameworks anchor the ingest: **Israel as a subset of [[canaan|Canaan]]** ("Canaanite" a geographic, not ethnic, label — a single cultural sphere of which Israel was one part) and **[[divine-patronage-ancient-near-east|divine patronage]]** as the common religion of the ANE (the patron–client structure projected onto a henotheistic pantheon; biblical "monotheism" really henotheism/monolatry). **4 new pages:** place [[canaan|Canaan]] (closing the gap the Dever ingest had to delink); concepts [[divine-patronage-ancient-near-east|Divine Patronage in the Ancient Near East]] and [[yahwism-and-israelite-religion|Yahwism and Israelite Religion]] (El→Baal→Yahweh; Kuntillet Ajrud, Ekron, Deir Alla); source page. **8 updated:** the three Levant controversies [[controversies/historicity-of-the-united-monarchy|United Monarchy]], [[controversies/emergence-of-early-israel|Emergence of Early Israel]], [[the-exodus|the Exodus]] (each now carries the moderate-skeptic reading beside Dever's centrism), plus [[biblical-archaeology]], [[philistines]], [[kingdom-of-israel]], [[kingdom-of-judah]], and period [[early-iron-age]]. Read as the skeptical-but-not-nihilist middle and the wiki's method primer. Top_100 #32 ✅. 0 broken links (changed set); schema clean. Filed to raw/2. Ancient & Classical Civilizations/Ancient Near East/. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8.

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/dever-beyond-the-texts-2017|William G. Dever, *Beyond the Texts: An Archaeological Portrait of Ancient Israel and Judah* (SBL Press, 2017)]] — the leading **archaeology-first** history of [[kingdom-of-israel|Israel]] and [[kingdom-of-judah|Judah]] (c. 1200–586 BCE), in which the Hebrew Bible is treated as a late, tendentious *secondary* source tested against the material record. Dever's magnum opus and the manifesto of a secular **[[biblical-archaeology|Syro-Palestinian archaeology]]**, written from a centrist position between biblical maximalists and the [[the-exodus|"minimalists"]], and against Finkelstein's Low Chronology. **Deployed Subagent Strategy** — three Sonnet agents over the substantive chapters (ch3 Emergence, ch4 Tenth Century, ch5–6 Iron IIB/IIC) drafted the two controversy pages + claim digests; the main thread authored the source page, the method/concept page, the Lachish page, and reconciliation. **4 new pages:** concept [[biblical-archaeology|Syro-Palestinian (Biblical) Archaeology]]; controversies [[controversies/emergence-of-early-israel|the Emergence of Early Israel]] (conquest / infiltration / peasant-revolt / sedentarization models; Merneptah's "Israel"; the collared-rim-ware / four-room-house / pig-avoidance marker debate) and [[controversies/historicity-of-the-united-monarchy|Historicity of the United Monarchy]] (minimalist vs. Low Chronology vs. centrist; six-chambered gates, Khirbet Qeiyafa, the Tel Dan "house of David" stele, Tel Reḥov C-14); place [[lachish|Lachish]] (the best-documented siege in the ancient Near East). **12 updated:** [[david]], [[solomon]], [[jerusalem]], [[hebrew-kingdom]], [[kingdom-of-israel]], [[kingdom-of-judah]], [[omride-dynasty]], [[hezekiah]], [[philistines]], events [[fall-of-samaria-722bce]] + [[sennacherib-siege-jerusalem-701bce]], period [[early-iron-age]]. Read critically as one strong, openly partisan voice in live debates. Top_100 #31 ✅. 0 broken links (changed set); schema clean. Filed to raw/2. Ancient & Classical Civilizations/Ancient Near East/. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 + subagents (A, B, C).

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/briant-from-cyrus-to-alexander-2002|Pierre Briant, *From Cyrus to Alexander: A History of the Persian Empire* (Eisenbrauns, 2002)]] — the **definitive single-author synthesis** of the [[achaemenid-empire|Achaemenid Empire]] (559–330 BCE), founding text of the "Achaemenid History" school, which writes the empire from its own evidence (Persepolis tablets, royal inscriptions, Babylonian/Egyptian/Aramaic documents) rather than through the hostile Greek tradition. **Deployed Subagent Strategy** — 8 Sonnet agents over disjoint chapter ranges (the ~40k-line body; the rest is Research Notes/Bibliography/Index) drafted grounded claim digests; the main thread authored the source page + Section Plan + Volume Synthesis Note, all new pages, and reconciliation. **8 new pages:** actors [[artaxerxes-i]], [[artaxerxes-iii]], [[darius-iii]], [[tissaphernes]]; process [[achaemenid-tribute-economy]]; concepts [[achaemenid-royal-ideology]] (builder/judge/warrior/gardener king under Ahura-Mazda), [[achaemenid-kings-men]] (the *bandaka* gift economy of the "Faithful"); place [[pasargadae]]. **9 updated:** [[achaemenid-empire]] (the Briant synthesis), [[cyrus-the-great]] (Cylinder-as-propaganda; forged Pasargadae inscriptions), [[cambyses]] (Serapeum/Udjahorresnet rehabilitation), [[darius-i]] (Behistun as self-justification; satrapy predates him), [[xerxes-i]] (against "decline begins with Xerxes"), [[artaxerxes-ii]] (Revolt of Satraps as local revolts; Anahita edict), [[gaumata-magush]] (Bardiya as legitimate claimant — [CONTRADICTION]), [[achaemenid-satrapy]], [[persian-army]] (against the mercenary-dependence myth). Three theses now thread the Achaemenid cluster: the **tribute + gift economy** as the real engine of empire; the **"declining/decadent empire" as a Hellenocentric myth**; and the **fall as contingent** (battlefield defeat + aristocratic defection), with [[alexander-the-great|Alexander]] as "the last of the Achaemenids." Top_100 #34 ✅. 0 broken links (changed set); schema clean (25 files). Filed to raw/2. Ancient & Classical Civilizations/Ancient Near East/. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 + subagents (1–8).

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/bryce-world-neo-hittite-kingdoms-2012|Trevor Bryce, *The World of the Neo-Hittite Kingdoms: A Political and Military History* (Oxford UP, 2012)]] — the **standard single-author political-military history** of the Syro-Hittite states, the Iron Age city-kingdoms of south-east Anatolia and north Syria that carried the [[hittites|Hittite]]-[[luwians|Luwian]] inheritance forward across the [[bronze-age-collapse-1200bce|Bronze Age Collapse]] until [[neo-assyrian-empire|Assyrian]] annexation. Complements Bryce's [[sources/bryce-kingdom-of-the-hittites-2005|Kingdom of the Hittites (2005)]] and [[sources/bryce-handbook-peoples-places-western-asia-2009|Handbook (2009)]]. **Deployed Subagent Strategy** — two Sonnet agents over the kingdom-heavy chapter blocks (ch5–6 Euphrates/Anti-Taurus; ch7–9 SE Anatolia/Aramaean) drafted the place pages; the main thread authored the source page, the Part I/III narrative, and reconciliation. **9 new pages:** places [[melid]] (Malatya/Arslantepe), [[kummukh]] (later Commagene), [[gurgum]] (Maraş — the most complete Neo-Hittite dynastic genealogy), [[unqi-pattin]] ('Amuq plain, Tell Ta'yinat), [[tabal]] (the fragmented plateau cluster), [[tuwana]] (Tyana, the İvriz relief), [[que-adanawa]] (Cilicia — the Karatepe & Çineköy bilinguals), [[bit-agusi-arpad]] (the major north-Syrian Aramaean state); concept [[biblical-hittites]] (why "Hittite" is a borrowed biblical label). **3 updated:** [[neo-hittite-states]] (continuity-vs-migration synthesis, member-kingdom index — Carchemish's surviving "Great King" line as the dynastic bridge), [[luwians]], period [[early-iron-age]] (the Syro-Hittite world corrects the "small states" theme for the northern Levant). Core argument: **continuity, not migration** — the states arose *in situ*, the apparent southward shift of "Hatti" reflecting the loss of the Anatolian heartland (aligns with [[sources/liverani-ancient-near-east-2014|Liverani]]). Top_100 #30 ✅. 0 broken links (changed files); schema clean. Filed to raw/2. Ancient & Classical Civilizations/Ancient Near East/. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 + subagents (A, B).

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/bryce-handbook-peoples-places-western-asia-2009|Trevor Bryce, *The Routledge Handbook of the Peoples and Places of Ancient Western Asia* (Routledge, 2009)]] — the wiki's **gazetteer-grade reference** for the peoples and places of ancient western Asia (Anatolia, Cyprus, Syria–Palestine, Mesopotamia, Iran, parts of Central Asia), a ~500,000-word A–Z encyclopedia of ~1,500 source-anchored entries from the Early Bronze Age to the fall of Persia (330 BCE), prefaced by a tight ~30-page **Historical Overview** of the whole region. Because the ANE is already one of the wiki's strongest areas (CAH + [[sources/liverani-ancient-near-east-2014|Liverani]] + [[sources/potts-archaeology-of-elam-2016|Potts]]), this was a **focused reference ingest** (per the hybrid-depth rule), not the subagent fleet: used to fill genuine page-level gaps and enrich existing pages, not to mine all entries. **5 new pages:** places [[eshnunna]] (the Diyala-basin Early/Middle Bronze Age kingdom), [[hamath]] (the Orontes [[neo-hittite-states|Neo-Hittite]]→[[arameans|Aramaean]] kingdom), [[persepolis]] (the Achaemenid ceremonial capital + find-spot of the Fortification Tablets); actors [[mannaea]] (the Iron Age Urmia-basin buffer fought over by [[neo-assyrian-empire|Assyria]] and [[urartu|Urartu]]), [[luwians]] (the Anatolian Indo-European people and the hieroglyphic script of the Neo-Hittite states). **9 updated:** Bryce's archaeology of the Hittite capital folded into the existing [[hattusha|Hattusha]] page (phased fortifications, Upper/Lower City, granaries, deliberate-abandonment), Bryce citation added to [[iamkhad|Yamhad]] (the kingdom of Aleppo); plus [[neo-assyrian-empire]], [[urartu]], [[achaemenid-empire]] (Persepolis), [[arameans]], [[neo-hittite-states]], [[amorites]], [[hittites]] ([[luwians]] link). **Dedup note:** the Hittite capital and the kingdom of Aleppo already existed under alternative transliterations (`hattusha`/`iamkhad`); near-miss duplicates `hattusa.md`/`yamhad.md` were caught and folded in (skill-obs #23 — search transliteration variants, not the chosen slug). Top_100 #28 ✅. 0 broken links (changed files). Filed to raw/2. Ancient & Classical Civilizations/Ancient Near East/. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8.

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/bryce-kingdom-of-the-hittites-2005|Trevor Bryce, *The Kingdom of the Hittites* (New Edition, Oxford UP, 2005)]] — the standard single-author **political-and-military history** of the Anatolian kingdom of Hatti, and the wiki's narrative spine for the [[hittites|Hittite]] Empire. **Deployed Subagent Strategy** (7 chapter-aligned disjoint body ranges, lines 740–15759; staggered Sonnet batches of 2 + sleeps; subagents created their owned reign/place pages and wrote range-exclusive grounded claim digests with verbatim Boğazköy-tablet quotes, main thread authored the spine + reconciled all pages). Its decisive contribution: **filling the previously-deferred Empire and Fall narrative** on [[hittites]] (the page formerly stopped at c. 1380 BCE and cross-referenced CAH for everything after). **22 new pages.** *Persons:* [[anitta]], [[labarna]], [[hantili-i]], [[telipinu]], [[tudhaliya-i-ii]], [[tudhaliya-iii]], [[urhi-teshub]], [[puduhepa]], [[tudhaliya-iv]], [[suppiluliuma-ii]], [[kurunta]]; *states/peoples:* [[arzawa]], [[kaska]]; *places:* [[kanesh]], [[kizzuwatna]], [[carchemish]], [[amurru]], [[tarhuntassa]], [[millawanda]], [[wilusa]]; *concept:* [[hittite-vassal-treaties]] (the LBA suzerain–vassal system, disambiguated from medieval [[vassalage]]); and the wiki's first Late-Bronze-Age **biography hub** [[hubs/biographies/late-bronze-age/shuppiluliuma-i|Šuppiluliuma I]] (~3,500 words). **22 updated:** [[hittites]] (Empire + Fall), [[actors/shuppiluliuma-i|shuppiluliuma-i]], the empire kings [[mursili-ii]]/[[muwatalli-ii]]/[[hattusili-iii]], Old-Kingdom [[hattusili-i]]/[[mursili-i]], [[mitanni]], [[sea-peoples]], [[neo-hittite-states]], [[assyria-middle]], [[new-kingdom-egypt]], [[ramesses-ii]], [[ugarit]], [[troy]], [[hattusha]], events [[bronze-age-collapse-1200bce]]/[[egyptian-hittite-treaty-1259bce]]/[[battle-of-qadesh-1274bce]] (+ the Qadesh warfare-hub tactical page, Hittite-side), the controversy [[ahhiyawa-question]], and the Biographies-Hub portal. Signature threads: **legitimacy manufactured after the fact** (Telipinu→Šuppiluliuma's fratricide→Hattusili III's *Apology*); **empire-by-treaty** (the graded vassal network + dynastic viceroyalty at Carchemish/Aleppo); the Hittite-archive face of **Qadesh** (tactical draw, strategic win) and the **Silver Treaty** (legitimacy over the Assyrian threat); the **Wilusa = Troy** identification and a political chronology for **Ahhiyawa**; and a **soft collapse** (Seeher's deliberate-abandonment of Hattusa; the Sea Peoples as a "disorganized array") that converges with [[sources/liverani-ancient-near-east-2014|Liverani]]. Middle chronology, explicitly provisional. Top_100 #29 ✅. 0 new broken links. Filed to raw/2. Ancient & Classical Civilizations/Ancient Near East/. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 + subagents (A1–A7).

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/potts-archaeology-of-elam-2016|D. T. Potts, *The Archaeology of Elam: Formation and Transformation of an Ancient Iranian State* (2nd ed., Cambridge, 2016)]] — the standard synthesis of **Elam** (southwestern Iran, late 4th millennium BCE–Sasanian era), and the spine source for the wiki's thin **Iran/Elam** coverage. **Deployed Subagent Strategy** (8 chapter-aligned disjoint body ranges, lines 770–22503; staggered Sonnet batches; subagents extracted range-exclusive grounded claim digests with verbatim quotes, main thread authored & reconciled all pages). Potts's governing thesis: **"Elam" is a Mesopotamian construct, not a continuous state** — there were "many Elams," and he explicitly **rejects the longue durée** for *la courte durée* (repeated dissolution and transformation), relocates the original centre to highland **[[anshan|Anshan]]** in Fars (after Vallat), and **severs [[proto-elamite|Proto-Elamite]] from historical Elam**. Its great value-add is extending coverage **past the old Bronze-Age terminus** into the previously-absent Neo-Elamite, Achaemenid, Elymaean and Sasanian phases. **10 new pages:** places [[anshan]], [[chogha-zanbil]] (the best-preserved Near Eastern ziggurat), [[gundeshapur]]; concept [[proto-elamite]]; actors [[puzur-inshushinak]], [[untash-napirisha]], [[shutruk-nahhunte-i]], [[shilhak-inshushinak-i]], [[elymais]]; event [[sack-of-susa-646bce]] (Assurbanipal, 646 BCE). **2 updated:** [[elam]] (hub extended with Neo-Elamite / Achaemenid / Elymais-Sasanian sections + the "many Elams" framing) and [[susa]] (rewritten with the full Susa I–Sasanian archaeological sequence). Signature threads: the highland–lowland "Anshan and Susa" structure; the Elamite language isolate and its undeciphered scripts; the **Elamite–Persian ethnogenesis** (Elamite as the Achaemenid chancery language); and the survival of "Elam" as name and identity for four millennia. Top_100 #33 ✅. 0 broken links. Filed to raw/2. Ancient & Classical Civilizations/Ancient Near East/. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 + subagents (1–8).

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/liverani-ancient-near-east-2014|Mario Liverani, *The Ancient Near East: History, Society and Economy* (Routledge, 2014; Italian orig. 1988)]] — Deployed Subagent Strategy (10 disjoint body ranges, lines 1107–28425; staggered Sonnet batches of 2 + sleeps; subagents extracted range-exclusive grounded claims with verbatim quotes, main thread authored and reconciled all pages). Liverani's classic single-author synthesis of three millennia (c. 3500–500 BCE) — overlaid as an **interpretive layer** on the wiki's already-strong (CAH-built) ANE backbone. Governing project: **"normalise" the ANE** (against the biblical, Orientalist, and "cradle of civilisation" myths) and read it through **society, economy, and ideology**, correcting the documentary bias toward palaces and literacy. **6 new pages:** concepts [[ancient-near-east-historiography]] (sources document ideology, not fact; centre/periphery propaganda; reading against the grain), [[ancient-near-eastern-modes-of-production]] (palace vs. household; redistribution/reciprocity; the "great organisations"), [[ancient-near-east-environment-and-society]] (interfaces/borders/niches; the fast/slow developmental models; migration scepticism; three technological phases); actors [[ebla]] (the Proto-Syrian kingdom — a clear prior gap), [[gutians]]; place [[arslantepe]] (the "citadel without a city"). **42 updated** across every period and the major actors/places/events of Mesopotamia, Syria, Anatolia, the Levant and Iran — incl. periods [[neolithic]]/[[chalcolithic]]/[[middle-bronze-age]]/[[late-bronze-age]]/[[early-iron-age]]; concepts [[urban-revolution]], [[temple-economy]], [[divine-kingship]], [[ancient-near-eastern-chronology]]; the Akkad–Ur III cluster ([[akkadian-empire]], [[sargon-of-akkad]], [[naram-sin]], [[third-dynasty-of-ur]], [[ur-nammu]], [[early-dynastic-sumer]]); MBA ([[amorites]], [[old-assyrian-trade]], [[mari]], [[hammurabi]], [[shamshi-adad-i]], [[hyksos]]); LBA ([[hittites]], [[mitanni]], [[assyria-middle]], [[ashur-uballit-i]], [[kassites]], [[elam]], [[ugarit]]); Iron Age ([[bronze-age-collapse-1200bce]], [[kingdom-of-israel]] (his minimalist reading), [[phoenicians]], [[arameans]], [[neo-hittite-states]], [[babylonia-iron-age]], [[chaldeans]]); and the empires ([[neo-assyrian-empire]], [[urartu]], [[medes]], [[neo-babylonian-empire]], [[achaemenid-empire]], [[fall-of-nineveh-612bce]], [[fall-of-babylon-539bce]]). Signature contributions: internal causes primary in the collapses (nomads as *effects*); the iron-and-alphabet "democratisation" of the Early Iron Age; the un-deified Hammurabi and the **demonstrative** (not prescriptive) law-code; the Conclusion's reading of 3500–500 BCE as a single mode of production closing at the **Axial Age**, with the real heirs of the ANE being the Hellenistic world, the Christian East, Iran, and Islam (not a Greece-Rome-Europe relay). Where his minimalist/structuralist positions diverge from the CAH spine (Israelite united monarchy, the "Median empire," collapse causation) they are flagged as one side of a live debate. Top_100 #27 ✅. 0 broken links. Filed to raw/2. Ancient & Classical Civilizations/Ancient Near East/. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 + subagents (A1–A8b).

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/effros-moreira-oxford-handbook-merovingian-world-2020|Bonnie Effros & Isabel Moreira (eds.), *The Oxford Handbook of the Merovingian World* (Oxford UP, 2020)]] — the reference survey of Merovingian studies (46 essays, ~1,100 pp.), ingested via the full **Deployed Subagent Strategy** (8 part-aligned disjoint body ranges, lines 1855–45120; staggered Sonnet batches of 2 + sleeps; subagents extracted range-exclusive grounded claim digests, main thread authored and reconciled all pages). Governing thesis: the Merovingian world was **connected, vital, and decentralized — not a "Dark Age,"** recovered by setting archaeology, bioarchaeology, numismatics, and archaeobotany *against* the texts. **37 new pages.** *Identity/society:* [[gender-in-merovingian-gaul]], [[merovingian-childhood]], [[merovingian-bioarchaeology]], [[migrants-and-minorities-in-merovingian-gaul]]. *Power:* [[merovingian-polity]], [[merovingian-queenship]], [[merovingian-army]], [[early-medieval-hospitals-and-charity]]. *Wider world:* [[merovingian-byzantine-relations]], [[cross-channel-exchange-britain-and-gaul]], [[early-slavs]]. *Written culture:* [[merovingian-historiography]], [[merovingian-legal-culture]], [[merovingian-hagiography]], [[merovingian-epistolary-culture]], [[merovingian-epigraphy]], [[venantius-fortunatus]], [[fredegar]]. *Landscapes:* [[merovingian-cities]], [[merovingian-rural-settlement]], [[end-of-the-roman-villa]], [[merovingian-religious-architecture]], [[farming-and-diet-in-merovingian-gaul]]. *Economy:* [[merovingian-emporia]], [[merovingian-coinage]], [[garnet-and-bead-trade]] (Sri Lankan/Indian garnets in Frankish cloisonné), [[merovingian-ceramics-and-mediterranean-trade]], [[merovingian-economy]] (Theuws's bottom-up "ritual economy"). *Religion:* [[merovingian-amulets-and-magic]], [[visions-and-the-afterlife-merovingian]], [[liturgical-commemoration-and-memoria]], [[merovingian-liturgy-and-lay-religion]], [[early-medieval-penance]], [[merovingian-christology]]; plus the field's own historiography ([[merovingian-archaeology]], [[merovingian-studies-historiography]]) and [[radegund-of-poitiers]]. **23 updated:** [[late-antiquity]], [[early-middle-ages]], [[ethnogenesis]], [[frankish-ethnogenesis]], [[merovingian-franks]], [[gregory-of-tours]], [[clovis]], [[francia]], [[rois-faineants]], [[salic-law]], [[brunhild]], [[fredegund]], [[merovingian-episcopate]], [[merovingian-monasticism]], [[columbanus]], [[avars]], [[ostrogoths]], [[lombards]], [[visigoths]], [[aquitaine]], [[battle-of-vouille-507]], [[roman-gaul]], [[byzantine-empire]]. Signature contributions: the case against the [[rois-faineants|rois fainéants]]; garnet provenancing placing Francia in Indian-Ocean trade; the bottom-up vs. elite-demand economic debate; and the recovery of Merovingian religion from the "theological void." Off-list source (not in Top_100). 0 new broken links. Filed to raw/3. Middle Ages/. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 + subagents (R1–R8).

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/rice-egypts-making-2003|Michael Rice, *Egypt's Making: The Origins of Ancient Egypt 5000–2000 BC* (2nd ed., Routledge, 2003)]] — **focused main-thread ingest**, not the subagent fleet: the topic (predynastic→Old Kingdom Egypt) was freshly scaffolded the same day by [[sources/shaw-oxford-history-ancient-egypt-2000|Shaw]] and [[sources/kemp-ancient-egypt-anatomy-2018|Kemp]], so per the hybrid-depth rule the ingest targeted **Rice's distinctive angles** rather than re-churning consensus. Rice is a semi-popular author with deep **Arabian-Gulf** field experience (he excavated the Barbar temple on Bahrain), and his value is twofold: a hard-pressed case for **Mesopotamian/Elamite/Dilmun contact** with late-predynastic Egypt, and a closing **Jungian/archetypal** reading of the Egyptian psyche. **2 new pages:** place [[naqada]] (the type-site Nubt and the Badarian→Naqada I/II/III sequence); controversy [[egypt-mesopotamia-predynastic-contact]] (the discarded "dynastic race" and diffusionist theories, the modern indigenous-state consensus, and Rice's trans-Arabian-route variant). **9 updated:** [[predynastic-egypt]] (Nabta Playa calendar circles, cattle/bull cult, Merimde/Maadi/Buto, SW-Asian contact), [[divine-kingship]] (the Dual Kingdom principle, king as divine aurochs, the Jungian reading as historiography), [[unification-of-egypt-3100bce]] (the votive palettes, Scorpion macehead, niched *serekh*), [[narmer]] (the eastern signature on the palette), [[djoser]] (Imhotep's titulary, Heliopolitan solar cult, deification), [[hierakonpolis]] (Falcon/Horus pole; the oval/Barbar parallel), [[abydos]] (retainer sacrifice; Khasekhemwy/Barbar masonry parallel), [[egyptian-dynastic-chronology]] (Rice's "Dynasty 0" table + Naqada–Ubaid synchronism), [[fall-of-old-kingdom-2160bce]] (structural decline). Filed as a **suggestive minority source**: presumptive weight to Shaw/Kemp on consensus; Rice carries the contact thesis and Arabian comparanda. Top_100 #25 ✅. Filed to raw/2. Ancient & Classical Civilizations/. 0 new broken links. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8.

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/barnes-archaeology-east-asia-2015|Gina L. Barnes, *Archaeology of East Asia: The Rise of Civilization in China, Korea and Japan* (Oxbow, 2015)]] — Deployed Subagent Strategy (7 weighted chapter-aligned disjoint body ranges, lines 1567–19219; staggered batches of 2 + 10s sleeps; Sonnet subagents extracted ~610 range-exclusive grounded claims with verbatim quotes, main thread authored all pages). The wiki's spine for **Korean and Japanese prehistory/protohistory** (its two weakest East Asian areas) and the only English synthesis treating China, Korea and Japan as **one connected system**. Built on Barnes's two coinages, now concept pages: the [[yellow-sea-interaction-sphere|Yellow Sea Interaction Sphere]] and [[pen-insular|"Pen/Insular"]] (Korea+Japan as one developmental zone), and her master claim of a **~2,000-year lag** in which the Pen/Insulae become [[pen-insular-state-formation|secondary states]] only after Mainland successor states collapse (3c AD). **19 new pages:** concepts [[yellow-sea-interaction-sphere]], [[pen-insular]]; processes [[peopling-of-east-asia]], [[rice-agriculture-spread-east-asia]], [[pen-insular-state-formation]], [[east-asian-bronze-metallurgy]]; place [[zhoukoudian]]; Korea [[chulmun-culture]], [[mumun-culture]], [[samhan]], [[puyo]], [[lelang-commandery]], [[koguryo]], [[paekche]], [[silla]], [[kaya]]; Japan [[jomon-culture]], [[yayoi-culture]], [[kofun-culture]]. **8 updated:** [[yamato-japan]] (secondary-state framing; Himiko→Nara Basin; "horse-rider conquest" rejected), [[korea-three-kingdoms]] (archaeological deep background), [[early-china-state-formation]] (definitional plurality; Liangzhu path; 4.2 kya collapse), [[shang-dynasty]] (capital sequence; "thin network" not territorial state), [[erlitou-culture]] (piece-mould Phase II), [[liangzhu-culture]] (Mojiaoshan/Fanshan; collapse), [[sanxingdui]] (non-Shang bronze world), [[anyang]] (Yinxu in the capital sequence). Signature framing: no indigenous chiefdoms in the Pen/Insulae while Mainland feasting/shaman ranking emerged; the [[mumun-culture|Mumun]]→[[yayoi-culture|Yayoi]] "package" by bilateral acculturation; [[lelang-commandery|Lelang]] as the sphere's hinge; even-handed treatment of the **Koguryo, Mimana and keyhole-tomb** nationalist controversies. Top_100 #19 ✅. Filed to raw/2. Ancient & Classical Civilizations/. 0 new broken links. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 + subagents (R1–R7).

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/manning-last-pharaohs-2010|J. G. Manning, *The Last Pharaohs: Egypt Under the Ptolemies, 305–30 BC* (Princeton UP, 2010)]] — Deployed Subagent Strategy (5 chapter-aligned disjoint body ranges, lines 582–9772; staggered batches of 2 + sleeps; Sonnet subagents extracted range-exclusive grounded claims with verbatim quotes, main thread authored all pages). The wiki's anchor for the **"institutional turn"** in Ptolemaic studies — a deliberate **third position** beside [[sources/shaw-oxford-history-ancient-egypt-2000|Shaw/Lloyd]] ("two cultures") and the CAH/Turner–Thompson Brussels-school revision: the Ptolemaic state as a **[[premodern-state|premodern state]] that governed "not over society, but rather through it,"** by **[[ptolemaic-state-formation|continual bargaining]]** with priesthoods, soldier-settlers, and local elites, on inherited [[saite-egypt|Saïte]]–[[achaemenid-egypt|Persian]] foundations. **5 new pages:** concepts [[new-institutional-economics]], [[premodern-state]], [[legal-pluralism]]; process [[ptolemaic-state-formation]]; event [[asyut-property-trial-170bce]] (the *laokritai* trial of 170 BCE, pSiut 1, from the Appendix). **7 updated:** [[ptolemaic-economy]] (Manning's "Position 3" — bargained state; critique of despotism/dirigisme/colonialism/banditry; now a 3-position controversy), [[ptolemaic-egypt]] (Manning section + reciprocal links), [[saite-egypt]] (Saïte state as institutional template), [[achaemenid-egypt]] (Persian foundation; Darius' law-"codification" as reauthorization), [[coinage]] (state-driven monetization + limits), [[ruler-cult]] (dynastic cult as bargaining instrument), [[egyptian-temple-economy]] (Ptolemaic subordination of the temples). Signature claims: a "hybrid" state with "roots planted firmly in the soil of Persian provincial rule"; "Egyptian and Greek law never merged. There was no Ptolemaic law"; the period as an **"efflorescence"** (extensive growth, no intensive per-capita growth). Top_100 #24 ✅. Filed to raw/2. Ancient & Classical Civilizations/. 0 new broken links. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 + subagents (R1–R5).

**New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/nelson-king-and-emperor-2019|Janet L. Nelson, *King and Emperor: A New Life of Charlemagne* (Allen Lane / University of California Press, 2019)]] — Deployed Subagent Strategy (5 chapter-aligned disjoint body ranges, lines 101–16970; staggered batches of 2 + 10s sleeps; Sonnet subagents extracted range-exclusive grounded claims with verbatim quotes, main thread authored all pages). Ingested **via the Biography Hub instructions**: the wiki's source spine for the life of Charles the Great and a sustained anti-teleological corrective to the Carolingian cluster. **Centerpiece:** [[hubs/biographies/early-middle-ages/charlemagne|Charlemagne — detailed biography]] (~5,400 words, full nine-section template; the wiki's first Early Middle Ages detailed bio). **10 new pages:** the detailed bio; place [[aachen]]; actors [[einhard]], [[hildegard]], [[fastrada]], [[tassilo-iii]], [[desiderius]], [[widukind]], [[pope-hadrian-i]], [[pope-leo-iii]]; event [[saxon-wars-772-804]]. **4 updated:** [[charlemagne]] (reciprocal bio link, birthdate corrected to **2 April 748**, Nelson historiography note), [[coronation-of-charlemagne-800]] (Nelson's "co-planned, not surprised" reading + *Romanum gubernans imperium* evasion), [[carolingian-conquest-of-lombard-italy-774]] (the "wergild" reading of the 14,000 solidi; bloodless conquest). Signature revisionism: refusal of "Charlemagne"/"Father of Europe" and reading the life *forward*; the queens [[hildegard]] & [[fastrada]] restored as political agents (*Friedelehe* rejected); the engineered fall of [[tassilo-iii|Tassilo III]]; scepticism on Verden's 4,500; rebuttal of Ganshof's "*l'échec de Charlemagne*" via a functioning late-imperial *missi*/capitulary system. Off-list source (not in Top_100). Filed to raw/3. Middle Ages/. 0 new broken links introduced. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 + subagents (R1–R5).

Signature contributions: **dynastic chronology as a political artefact** (Manetho/king-lists/Sothic); the **intermediate periods reframed** (FIP "dark age" revisionism promoted to a controversy; Hyksos as Levantine immigrants; Libyans as a chiefdom-federation); **Egyptian imperialism as a two-model structure** (colonial Nubia vs. Levantine vassalage). Dedup conformed to existing transliteration slugs (mentuhotpe/senwosret/naucratis) and folded 6 would-be duplicates into existing pages (skill-obs #13). Top_100 #23 ✅. Filed to raw/2. Geary, *Before France and Germany: The Creation and Transformation of the Merovingian World* (Oxford UP, 1988)]] — Deployed Subagent Strategy (4 weighted chapter-aligned disjoint body ranges, lines 270–9037; staggered batches of 2 + 10s sleeps; Sonnet subagents extracted range-exclusive grounded claims, main thread authored all pages). The wiki's spine for the **Merovingian world**: the barbarians, and the Franks above all, as *a creation of Rome*; ethnogenesis over migration; transformation, not fall. **22 new pages:** concepts [[comitatus]], [[salic-law]], [[rois-faineants]]; processes [[barbarization-of-the-roman-army]], [[frankish-ethnogenesis]], [[merovingian-monasticism]], [[christianisation-of-the-frankish-aristocracy]]; actors [[visigoths]], [[ostrogoths]], [[brunhild]], [[fredegund]], [[chlothar-ii]], [[dagobert-i]], [[pippinids]], [[merovingian-episcopate]]; places [[francia]], [[austrasia]], [[neustria]], [[aquitaine]], [[provence]], [[bavaria]]; event [[battle-of-vouille-507]]. **12 updated:** [[clovis]] (Syagrius coup; conversion as collective/political act; *civitas*-boundary partition), [[childeric]] (last "imperial German"), [[merovingian-franks]] (no legal nobility; ~2% Frankish; minorities not degeneracy), [[gregory-of-tours]] (episcopal kin-network), [[columbanus]] (expanded from skeleton), [[charles-martel]] (church-office takeover mechanism), [[gothic-peoples]] (Tervingi/Greuthungi ethnogenesis), [[ethnogenesis]] (Geary's Anglophone reception), [[roman-gaul]] (late-Roman transformation), [[battle-of-tertry-687]] ("after Tertry" consolidation), periods [[late-antiquity]]/[[early-middle-ages]]. Signature framing: the [[rois-faineants|rois fainéants]] as manufactured Carolingian propaganda; the orthodox-Christian Frankish **amalgamation** vs the Arian Gothic kingdoms; the seventh-century social revolution as the [[pippinids|Pippinid]] capture of the Church. Off-list source (not in Top_100). Filed to raw/3. Middle Ages/. 0 new broken links introduced. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 + subagents (R1–R4).

- **2026-06-27**: Ghazarian, *The Armenian Kingdom in Cilicia During the Crusades* (2000) — Top_100 #86 (gap region: Armenia/Caucasus, Crusades era). **New (2026-06-27):** [[sources/phillipson-foundations-african-civilisation-2012|David W. Phillipson, *Foundations of an African Civilisation: Aksum and the Northern Horn, 1000 BC – AD 1300* (James Currey, 2012)]] — the authoritative synthesis of Aksumite archaeology, epigraphy, numismatics, and linguistics. Phillipson's overarching argument: **Aksumite civilisation was fundamentally indigenous**, not a South Arabian colonial implant; South Arabian script, architectural forms, and religious vocabulary were selectively adopted by local elites into an existing developmental trajectory. Covers 1000 BC–AD 1300 in three parts: pre-Aksumite background (D'MT, Yeha, first-millennium-BC continuity); the Aksumite kingdom at its peak (Ezana's conversion, coinage, stelae, trade, religion); and its post-Aksumite transformation (eastern Tigray church-builders, Zagwe dynasty, Lalibela). **Deployed Subagent Strategy** — 4 Sonnet agents over disjoint chapter-ranges; main thread scaffolded, reconciled, and committed. **25 new pages:** events [[aksumite-christianisation-340ce]], [[aksumite-conquest-kush-350ce]], [[aksumite-control-yemen-520ce]], [[aksumite-decline-700ce]], [[aksumite-stelae-erection-3c-ce]]; concepts [[aksumite-architecture]], [[aksumite-coinage]], [[aksumite-kingship]], [[aksumite-legacy]], [[aksumite-phasing-scheme]], [[aksumite-pre-christian-religion]], [[aksumite-stelae]], [[ge-ez-script]], [[pre-aksumite-period]], [[south-arabian-influence-horn]]; processes [[aksumite-expansion]], [[aksumite-state-formation]], [[aksumite-trade-network]]; actors [[dmt-kingdom]], [[ezana-of-aksum]], [[frumentius]], [[zagwe-dynasty]], [[zoscales]]; places [[aksum-city]], [[adulis]], [[hawelti-melazo]], [[lalibela]], [[ona-nagast]], [[yeha]]; controversies [[aksumite-decline-causes]] (Reader's environmental-collapse vs. Phillipson's political-transformation thesis). **6 updated:** [[aksum]], [[dmt-kingdom]], [[ezana-of-aksum]], [[zagwe-dynasty]], [[aksum-city]], [[adulis]]. Top_100 #87 ✅. 0 new broken links (changed set); schema clean. Filed to raw/4. Modern Times/. Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.6 + subagents (1–4).

- **2026-06-27**: Wickham, *Medieval Europe* (Yale, 2016) — Chris Wickham's one-volume **materialist, comparative interpretation** of 500–1500. Ingested **distinctive-scholarship** (topic strongly covered; the payload is the analytic frame). The spine: the **[[concepts/politics-of-land|politics of land]]** — power flows from controlling land and the peasant surplus, and a state's *form* (tax-based vs. land-based) sets its solidity and its tendency to fragment. Key theses captured: the **end of the Roman fiscal system** as the foundational early-medieval fact; **Carolingian Francia** as a public-politics "experiment" without taxation; the **eleventh-century "feudal revolution"** as the great post-Roman break (bounded, cellular local power); the **late Middle Ages as a strengthening public sphere**, not a "waning"; and **[[events/fourth-crusade-1204|1204]]** as "the failure of alternatives" (the destruction of the tax-based Byzantine model). New (2): [[concepts/politics-of-land]], [[sources/wickham-medieval-europe-2016]]. Updated (6): [[controversies/feudal-revolution-debate]] (Wickham's qualified mutationism), [[events/fourth-crusade-1204]], [[periods/early-middle-ages]], [[periods/high-middle-ages]], [[periods/late-middle-ages]]. Schema clean; 0 new broken links. Top_100 #71 ✅.

- **2026-06-27**: Laiou & Mottahedeh (eds.), *The Crusades from the Perspective of Byzantium and the Muslim World* (Dumbarton Oaks, 2001) — Dumbarton Oaks symposium proceedings; 12 specialist essays reconstructing the Crusades from non-western perspectives. **Deployed Subagent Strategy** (5 Sonnet agents, essay-boundary chunks). Central findings: (1) Kolbaba — anti-Latin Byzantine polemic was driven by internal theological disputes (azymes, filioque), not the Crusades directly; (2) Constable — western crusade historiography organized into three periods, the traditionalist/pluralist definitional debate unresolved; (3) Dennis — Byzantine wars were "just wars" not "holy wars" by any definition; (4) El-Cheikh — Arabic sources learned to distinguish *al-Rum* from *al-Ifranj* as Crusades progressed; (5) Grabar — Crusades were an "impulse of energy, not of forms" for Islamic art. - **2026-06-27**: Miles, *Carthage Must Be Destroyed: The Rise and Fall of an Ancient Civilization* (Viking/Penguin, 2010) — a cultural, religious, and historiographical history of Carthage, layered over the wiki's existing Punic military backbone (Goldsworthy + Warfare Hub). Two organizing theses: (1) Carthage's history was written by its Roman destroyers (*fides Punica* as ideological construct, the libraries dispersed in 146 BCE); (2) the **Heracles–Melqart syncretism** as the contested ideological currency of the western Mediterranean, weaponized by Hannibal's "footsteps of Heracles" campaign and ultimately captured by Rome and Augustus. **Deployed Subagent Strategy** (3 Sonnet agents over disjoint chapter ranges; claim digests reconciled and authored on the main thread). - **2026-06-27**: Bashford & Macintyre (eds.), *The Cambridge History of Australia, Vol. 1: Indigenous and Colonial Australia* (Cambridge UP, 2013) — the authoritative reference for Australia from the deep Indigenous past through Federation (1901); the **first substantial backbone for the Australia gap**, paired with [[sources/denoon-cambridge-pacific-islanders-1997|Denoon's Pacific Islanders]]. **Deployed Subagent Strategy** (9 Sonnet agents over disjoint chapter-clusters of the 23 chapters / 2 parts; main thread scaffolded the source page + Section Plan + 8 anchor pages, then reconciled — resolving an event/process slug collision in favour of the [[processes/australian-frontier-conflict|frontier-conflict process page]] — and linted to 0 broken links). Controlling thesis: a **"geographic sensibility"** with **land** as the master theme binding [[concepts/terra-nullius]], [[processes/australian-pastoral-expansion|pastoral expansion]], and [[processes/australian-frontier-conflict|frontier dispossession]] under the logic of [[concepts/settler-colonialism|settler colonialism]]; plus an **anti-isolationism** relocating colonial Australia inside "[[concepts/greater-britain|Greater Britain]]", the [[processes/australian-pacific-relations|Asia-Pacific]], and a [[processes/australia-maritime-world|maritime world]], with [[events/australian-federation-1901|Federation]] read as empire's "confident vanguard". **37 new pages.** Deep past: [[processes/deep-aboriginal-history]], [[concepts/fire-stick-farming]], [[processes/macassan-australian-contact]], [[events/first-european-encounters-australia]]. - **2026-06-27**: Salomon & Schwartz (eds.), *The Cambridge History of the Native Peoples of the Americas, Vol. III: South America, Part 2* (Cambridge UP, 1999) — the colonial-to-contemporary continuation of [[sources/cambridge-history-native-peoples-americas-v3-1|Part 1]], **closing the post-conquest half of the South America gap** (chs 14–26: the colonial Andean and La Plata heartlands, the unconquered margins, the 18th-c. rebellions, 19th-c. nation-making, and the 20th-c. indigenous resurgence). **Deployed Subagent Strategy** (8 Sonnet agents over disjoint chapter ranges; main thread scaffolded the source page + Section Plan + 5 anchors, then reconciled — merging a guaycurú duplicate, creating 8 reconciliation anchors, de-linking ~30 non-page targets — and linted to 0 broken links). Controlling thesis: native South American history continues **through** colony and nation as **adaptive persistence**, not disappearance; the [[concepts/reduccion|reducción]]/[[concepts/mita|mita]]/[[concepts/colonial-andean-tribute|tribute]]/[[concepts/hacienda-system|hacienda]] as arenas of struggle; the **heartland/margins** distinction ([[processes/araucania-frontier-1573-1882|Araucanía]], [[processes/pampas-horse-cultures|pampas]], [[processes/chaco-frontier-1573-1882|Chaco]], [[processes/western-amazonia-colonial-1540-1820|western Amazonia]], [[processes/northeastern-south-america-colonial-1500-1900|Orinoco-Guiana]]); **ethnogenesis** ([[processes/south-american-ethnogenesis-colonial]]) generating new peoples; and the 20th-c. shift from [[concepts/indigenismo|indigenismo]] to self-representation ([[concepts/katarismo|katarismo]], [[actors/conaie|CONAIE]], [[processes/south-american-indigenous-rights-movement]]). **50 new pages** — incl. processes [[processes/la-plata-basin-colonial-invasion-1535-1650]], [[processes/colonial-andean-heartland-1570-1780]], [[processes/republic-of-indians-in-revolt]], [[processes/andean-peasants-nation-making-19th-century]], [[processes/amazon-rubber-boom]], [[processes/andean-peoples-twentieth-century]], [[processes/lowland-peoples-twentieth-century]]; events [[events/tupac-amaru-rebellion-1780]], [[events/guarani-jesuit-missions]], [[events/siege-of-la-paz-1781]], [[events/putumayo-atrocities]], [[events/agrarian-reform-bolivia-1953]]; actors [[actors/guarani]], [[actors/bandeirantes]], [[actors/toledo-francisco-de]], [[actors/tupac-amaru-jose-gabriel]], [[actors/tupaj-katari]], [[actors/society-of-jesus]]; concepts [[concepts/reduccion]], [[concepts/repartimiento-de-mercancias]], [[concepts/debt-peonage]], [[concepts/hacienda-system]]; places [[places/asuncion]], [[places/mainas-mission]], [[places/guiana-orinoco-region]]. - **2026-06-27**: Hutton, *Blood and Mistletoe: The History of the Druids in Britain* (Yale UP, 2009) — the definitive **reception history of the Druids in Britain c. 1500–present**: how Britons (Scots, English, Welsh) imagined, appropriated, and reinvented the ancient Druids, *not* a study of the Iron Age Druids themselves (which remain the wiki's [[concepts/druids|Druids and Celtic Religion]] page). **Deployed Subagent Strategy** (4 Sonnet agents over disjoint chapter ranges; main thread scaffolded source page + Section Plan + the process/actor/concept/controversy anchors first, then reconciled — integrating continental-origin and convivial-club claims from agent claims files, de-linking a non-existent Royal Society page — and linted to 0 broken links). Controlling thesis: interest in Druids is intrinsic to **modernity and cultural nationalism** ("Thinking with Druids") — booming with the Renaissance, peaking in a "**Druidic century**" c. 1740–1860 when the new British superstate needed a shared pre-national past, then displaced by the [[concepts/three-age-system|three-age system]] — driven by three semi-disreputable catalysts ([[actors/stukeley-william|Stukeley]], [[actors/morganwg-iolo|Iolo Morganwg]], [[actors/reid-george-watson|Reid]]) and diverging sharply between the Scottish, English, and Welsh nations. **15 new pages.** Process: [[processes/druid-revival]]. **New (2026-07-03):** [[sources/bonner-first-pariah-state-2026|Robert E. Bonner, *The First Pariah State: How the Proslavery Confederacy Menaced the World* (Princeton University Press, 2026)]] — the first sustained scholarly treatment of the 1861–1865 transatlantic campaign to stigmatize and isolate the Confederacy as a uniquely dangerous "new power of a new species" (Sumner) and prototype pariah state. Centers the high-seas theater (Semmes, Alabama/Florida commerce raiding, privateers, slave-trade revival fears) and the verbatim self-incrimination of Confederate leaders (Stephens Cornerstone Speech as the single most damaging document). Strong on public diplomacy (Sumner Cooper Union speech, Bright, Laboulaye, Black abolitionist orators in Europe), international-law debates (recognition, belligerency, piracy), and the post-1865 reversal into Lost Cause melodrama (especially France). **Deployed Subagent Strategy (two-stage)**: main-thread scaffold + duplicate pre-scan + 3 disjoint body caches; 3 Sonnet agents wrote claims files only; main thread created 12 pages (source + 5 actors including Davis/Stephens/Semmes/Sumner/Adams + cornerstone event + pariah + lost-cause concepts + yancey/laboulaye) and updated the core [[events/american-civil-war-1861-1865]] with a new "International Pariah Campaign" section. 0 new broken links (stash + targeted verification). Source filed to raw/4. Modern Times/. Fills documented post-1750 Americas + diplomatic gap. Co-Authored-By: main thread + 3 Sonnet subagents.


**New (2026-07-03):** [[sources/anderson-che-guevara-revolutionary-life-1997|Jon Lee Anderson, *Che Guevara: A Revolutionary Life* (Grove Press, 1997; rev. 2010)]] — the standard scholarly biography, based on unprecedented access (Aleida March, unpublished diaries, Bolivian participants including the 1995 confession that enabled the 1997 exhumation). **Deployed Subagent Strategy (two-stage for well-trodden Cold War topic)**: main-thread scaffold (source page + Section Plan; pre-created canonical anchors guevara-ernesto-che, castro-fidel, cuban-revolution-1959, focoism, hombre-nuevo, bio hub + granma/death events); 6 disjoint caches; 4 Sonnet subagents (claims files only) + main-thread recovery of La Cabaña + Bolivia execution ranges. **9 created**: source; actors (guevara-ernesto-che, castro-fidel); events (cuban-revolution-1959, granma-landing-1956, death-of-che-guevara-1967); concepts (focoism, hombre-nuevo); hubs/biographies/cold-war/guevara-ernesto-che. **6+ updated**: cold-war, places/cuba, actor pages (major), revolution event, concepts. Key grounded content: birth deception/asthma/travels/Guatemala; Granma disaster & Alegría de Pío (ammunition choice, fatalism); Eutimio execution; Mompié purge; La Cabaña; new man ideology; 1965 farewell; Congo/Bolivia failures; capture/execution at La Higuera (Rodríguez/CIA, hands amputated, secret burial); myth vs. man. 0 new broken links (verified). Fills post-1750 Americas gap. Source filed to raw/4. Modern Times/. Co-Authored-By: main thread + 4 Sonnet subagents.

**New (2026-07-03):** [[sources/isaacson-benjamin-franklin-2003|Walter Isaacson, *Benjamin Franklin: An American Life* (Simon & Schuster, 2003)]] — the standard one-volume popular biography of the polymath Founding Father. **Deployed Subagent Strategy**: main-thread scaffold (source page + Section Plan; identified the stub actor page for expansion + the age-of-expansion bio-hub slot alongside Adams/Jefferson/Washington/Hamilton), 5 disjoint chapter-group caches, 5 Sonnet subagents to scratchpad digests, main-thread gap-read of the 1785 return/PA-presidency stretch, synthesis on the main thread. **1 created**: [[hubs/biographies/age-of-expansion/benjamin-franklin]] (~5,000-word detailed biography). **1 updated**: [[actors/benjamin-franklin]] (stub → full network summary; period corrected early-modern→age-of-expansion). Key grounded content: dissenting-artisan formation + self-taught prose/argument methods; print/media full-stack + retirement at 42; electricity/kite/lightning rod/Copley Medal; associational civic institutions; Albany Plan; the royalist-to-rebel arc through the Hutchinson letters and the 1774 Cockpit; the "self-evident" edit; the 1778 French alliance and 1783 Treaty of Paris; the 1787 Convention and Great Compromise; late antislavery; the William rupture. Isaacson's "invention of America" thesis quarantined as authorial interpretation. Source filed to raw/4. Modern Times/. Co-Authored-By: main thread + 5 Sonnet subagents.
